<issue_description>
Requesting loop_scope="module" (or "session" etc.) returns incorrect event_loop if another test function requests a different loop.
First of all, thanks for the great pytest plugin! 
Also, I like the idea of being able to specify event_loop scopes explicitly and separately to the fixture scopes. 

While playing with this, I kept running into issues that my "session" scoped event_loop seemed to change between tests that were running in different files, but only if certain other tests were run... 

I think this example reproduces the issues in a single file.  I recognize that the docs explicitly advise against doing this, but I hope it makes the problem easier to reason about.

Here's the example.

```python
import pytest_asyncio
import asyncio
import pytest


class FakeAsyncConnection:
    def __init__(self, loop):
        self.loop = loop

    async def do_something(self):
        # Check if the current loop is the same as the one with which the
        #  connection was created
        if asyncio.get_event_loop() is not self.loop:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "This connection is being used with a different event loop!")
        return "Success"


@pytest_asyncio.fixture(scope="module", loop_scope="module")
async def async_connection():
    """Set up a async connection object with module scope."""
    event_loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    print(f"Setting up fixture: event_loop_id {id(event_loop)}")
    connection = FakeAsyncConnection(event_loop)
    yield connection


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use module loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_2(async_connection):
    """Use module loop again"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="function")
async def test_use_function_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use function loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="This connection is being used with a different event loop!"):
        # This should raise an error because the connection is being used with a different loop
        await async_connection.do_something()


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_3(async_connection):
    """Unexpectedly fail to use module scope again"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"
```

I would expect all tests to pass, however, the final test `test_use_module_scope_loop_3` fails **only** if the `test_use_function_scope_loop_1` is present. If the function scope one is commented out, the final test does pass (as expected).

The fixtures aren't obviously set up incorrectly (running with `--setup-show`):
```
SETUP    S event_loop_policy
    SETUP    M tests/test_a.py::<event_loop> (fixtures used: event_loop_policy)
    SETUP    M async_connection
        tests/test_a.py::test_use_module_scope_loop_1 (fixtures used: async_connection, event_loop_policy, request, tests/test_a.py::<event_loop>).
        tests/test_a.py::test_use_module_scope_loop_2 (fixtures used: async_connection, event_loop_policy, request, tests/test_a.py::<event_loop>).
        SETUP    F event_loop
        tests/test_a.py::test_use_function_scope_loop_1 (fixtures used: async_connection, event_loop, event_loop_policy, request).
        TEARDOWN F event_loop
        tests/test_a.py::test_use_module_scope_loop_3 (fixtures used: async_connection, event_loop_policy, request, tests/test_a.py::<event_loop>)F
    TEARDOWN M async_connection
    TEARDOWN M tests/test_a.py::<event_loop>
TEARDOWN S event_loop_policy
```

But for some reason I don't understand, the module scoped event loop changes for the last test.


The printed loop ids tell the same story... The fixture and first two tests all get the the same loop_id as expected. The function scope test gets a new one as expected. Then the final module scope test also gets a new loop_id (different from both previous loop_ids) unexpectedly. 

Versions:
python: 3.12.7
pytest: 8.3.3
pytest-asyncio: 0.24.0

Also, my pytest settings are:
```
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
asyncio_mode = "auto"
asyncio_default_fixture_loop_scope="function"
```
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue description:

1. The problem involves pytest-asyncio's loop_scope feature
2. When requesting loop_scope="module" (or "session"), it returns incorrect event_loop if another test function requests a different loop
3. The key scenario:
   - Tests with loop_scope="module" share the same event loop correctly
   - But when a test with loop_scope="function" runs, it somehow affects subsequent module-scoped tests
   - Specifically, test_use_module_scope_loop_3 fails because it gets a different event loop than the fixture was created with

Key technical details:
- pytest-asyncio version: 0.24.0
- pytest version: 8.3.3  
- Python version: 3.12.7
- Configuration: asyncio_mode = "auto", asyncio_default_fixture_loop_scope="function"
- Error: RuntimeError("This connection is being used with a different event loop!")

The problem seems to be that the event loop caching/matching mechanism isn't properly respecting the loop_scope boundaries when different scopes are mixed.

Best practices mentioned:
- The docs advise against mixing different loop scopes
- But the user wants to be able to specify event_loop scopes explicitly and separately from fixture scopes</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes a problem with pytest-asyncio's `loop_scope` feature where:

**Technical Details:**
- **Method/Feature**: `loop_scope` parameter in pytest-asyncio fixtures and test markers
- **Error Message**: `RuntimeError("This connection is being used with a different event loop!")`
- **Configuration**: `asyncio_mode = "auto"`, `asyncio_default_fixture_loop_scope="function"`
- **Versions**: pytest-asyncio 0.24.0, pytest 8.3.3, Python 3.12.7

**Problem Explanation:**
When tests with different `loop_scope` values are mixed, the module/session-scoped event loops are not properly maintained. Specifically:
1. Module-scoped tests correctly share the same event loop initially
2. A function-scoped test introduces a new event loop 
3. Subsequent module-scoped tests get a completely different event loop (not the original module one)
4. This breaks fixtures that expect to maintain the same event loop throughout their scope

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a fixture with `scope="module"` and `loop_scope="module"`
2. Run multiple tests with `@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")` 
3. Run a test with `@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="function")`
4. Run another test with `@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")`
5. The final test fails because it uses a different event loop than the fixture

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing/Fixing
- Need to understand how pytest-asyncio manages event loop caching per scope
- Should respect fixture scope boundaries when managing event loops
- Must ensure that same `loop_scope` values consistently return the same event loop within that scope
- Fix should be minimal and not break existing functionality

    17	`pytest-asyncio <https://pytest-asyncio.readthedocs.io/en/latest/>`_ is a `pytest <https://docs.pytest.org/en/latest/contents.html>`_ plugin. It facilitates testing of code that uses the `asyncio <https://docs.python.org/3/library/asyncio.html>`_ library.
    18	
    19	Specifically, pytest-asyncio provides support for coroutines as test functions. This allows users to *await* code inside their tests. For example, the following code is executed as a test item by pytest:
    20	
    21	.. code-block:: python
    22	
    23	    @pytest.mark.asyncio
    24	    async def test_some_asyncio_code():
    25	        res = await library.do_something()
    26	        assert b"expected result" == res
    27	
    28	More details can be found in the `documentation <https://pytest-asyncio.readthedocs.io/en/latest/>`_.
    29	
    30	Note that test classes subclassing the standard `unittest <https://docs.python.org/3/library/unittest.html>`__ library are not supported. Users
    31	are advised to use `unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase <https://docs.python.org/3/library/unittest.html#unittest.IsolatedAsyncioTestCase>`__
    32	or an async framework such as `asynctest <https://asynctest.readthedocs.io/en/latest>`__.
    33	
    34	
    35	pytest-asyncio is available under the `Apache License 2.0 <https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest-asyncio/blob/main/LICENSE>`_.
    36	
    37	
    38	Installation
    39	------------
    40	
    41	To install pytest-asyncio, simply:
    42	
    43	.. code-block:: bash
    44	
    45	    $ pip install pytest-asyncio
    46	
    47	This is enough for pytest to pick up pytest-asyncio.
    48	
    49	
    50	Contributing
    51	------------
    52	Contributions are very welcome. Tests can be run with ``tox``, please ensure
    53	the coverage at least stays the same before you submit a pull request.

  1102	def _retrieve_scope_root(item: Collector | Item, scope: str) -> Collector:
  1103	    node_type_by_scope = {
  1104	        "class": Class,
  1105	        "module": Module,
  1106	        "package": Package,
  1107	        "session": Session,
  1108	    }
  1109	    scope_root_type = node_type_by_scope[scope]
  1110	    for node in reversed(item.listchain()):
  1111	        if isinstance(node, scope_root_type):
  1112	            assert isinstance(node, pytest.Collector)
  1113	            return node
  1114	    error_message = (
  1115	        f"{item.name} is marked to be run in an event loop with scope {scope}, "
  1116	        f"but is not part of any {scope}."
  1117	    )
  1118	    raise pytest.UsageError(error_message)
  1119

  1121	@pytest.fixture
  1122	def event_loop(request: FixtureRequest) -> Iterator[asyncio.AbstractEventLoop]:
  1123	    """Create an instance of the default event loop for each test case."""
  1124	    new_loop_policy = request.getfixturevalue(event_loop_policy.__name__)
  1125	    asyncio.set_event_loop_policy(new_loop_policy)
  1126	    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop_policy().new_event_loop()
  1127	    # Add a magic value to the event loop, so pytest-asyncio can determine if the
  1128	    # event_loop fixture was overridden. Other implementations of event_loop don't
  1129	    # set this value.
  1130	    # The magic value must be set as part of the function definition, because pytest
  1131	    # seems to have multiple instances of the same FixtureDef or fixture function
  1132	    loop.__original_fixture_loop = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
  1133	    yield loop

   658	_event_loop_fixture_id = StashKey[str]()
   659	_fixture_scope_by_collector_type: Mapping[type[pytest.Collector], _ScopeName] = {
   660	    Class: "class",
   661	    # Package is a subclass of module and the dict is used in isinstance checks
   662	    # Therefore, the order matters and Package needs to appear before Module
   663	    Package: "package",
   664	    Module: "module",
   665	    Session: "session",
   666	}
   667	
   668	# A stack used to push package-scoped loops during collection of a package
   669	# and pop those loops during collection of a Module
   670	__package_loop_stack: list[FixtureFunctionMarker | FixtureFunction] = []
   671	
   672	
   673	@pytest.hookimpl
   674	def pytest_collectstart(collector: pytest.Collector) -> None:
   675	    try:
   676	        collector_scope = next(
   677	            scope
   678	            for cls, scope in _fixture_scope_by_collector_type.items()
   679	            if isinstance(collector, cls)
   680	        )
   681	    except StopIteration:
   682	        return
   683	    # Session is not a PyCollector type, so it doesn't have a corresponding
   684	    # "obj" attribute to attach a dynamic fixture function to.
   685	    # However, there's only one session per pytest run, so there's no need to
   686	    # create the fixture dynamically. We can simply define a session-scoped
   687	    # event loop fixture once in the plugin code.
   688	    if collector_scope == "session":
   689	        event_loop_fixture_id = _session_event_loop.__name__
   690	        collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id
   691	        return
   692	    # There seem to be issues when a fixture is shadowed by another fixture
   693	    # and both differ in their params.
   694	    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/2043
   695	    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/11350
   696	    # As such, we assign a unique name for each event_loop fixture.
   697	    # The fixture name is stored in the collector's Stash, so it can
   698	    # be injected when setting up the test
   699	    event_loop_fixture_id = f"{collector.nodeid}::<event_loop>"
   700	    collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id
   701	
   702	    @pytest.fixture(
   703	        scope=collector_scope,
   704	        name=event_loop_fixture_id,
   705	    )
   706	    def scoped_event_loop(
   707	        *args,  # Function needs to accept "cls" when collected by pytest.Class
   708	        event_loop_policy,
   709	    ) -> Iterator[asyncio.AbstractEventLoop]:
   710	        new_loop_policy = event_loop_policy
   711	        with _temporary_event_loop_policy(new_loop_policy):
   712	            loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()
   713	            loop.__pytest_asyncio = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   714	            asyncio.set_event_loop(loop)
   715	            yield loop
   716	            loop.close()
   717	
   718	    # @pytest.fixture does not register the fixture anywhere, so pytest doesn't
   719	    # know it exists. We work around this by attaching the fixture function to the
   720	    # collected Python object, where it will be picked up by pytest.Class.collect()
   721	    # or pytest.Module.collect(), respectively
   722	    if type(collector) is Package:
   723	        # Packages do not have a corresponding Python object. Therefore, the fixture
   724	        # for the package-scoped event loop is added to a stack. When a module inside
   725	        # the package is collected, the module will attach the fixture to its
   726	        # Python object.
   727	        __package_loop_stack.append(scoped_event_loop)
   728	    elif isinstance(collector, Module):
   729	        # Accessing Module.obj triggers a module import executing module-level
   730	        # statements. A module-level pytest.skip statement raises the "Skipped"
   731	        # OutcomeException or a Collector.CollectError, if the "allow_module_level"
   732	        # kwargs is missing. These cases are handled correctly when they happen inside
   733	        # Collector.collect(), but this hook runs before the actual collect call.
   734	        # Therefore, we monkey patch Module.collect to add the scoped fixture to the
   735	        # module before it runs the actual collection.
   736	        def _patched_collect():
   737	            # If the collected module is a DoctestTextfile, collector.obj is None
   738	            module = collector.obj
   739	            if module is not None:
   740	                module.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop
   741	                try:
   742	                    package_loop = __package_loop_stack.pop()
   743	                    module.__pytest_asyncio_package_scoped_event_loop = package_loop
   744	                except IndexError:
   745	                    pass
   746	            return collector.__original_collect()
   747	
   748	        collector.__original_collect = collector.collect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   749	        collector.collect = _patched_collect  # type: ignore[method-assign]
   750	    elif isinstance(collector, Class):
   751	        collector.obj.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop
   752	
   753	
   754	@contextlib.contextmanager
   755	def _temporary_event_loop_policy(policy: AbstractEventLoopPolicy) -> Iterator[None]:
   756	    old_loop_policy = asyncio.get_event_loop_policy()
   757	    try:
   758	        old_loop = _get_event_loop_no_warn()
   759	    except RuntimeError:
   760	        old_loop = None

Let me create a reproduction script based on the issue description:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pytest-dev__pytest-asyncio__0.25/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>import pytest_asyncio
import asyncio
import pytest


class FakeAsyncConnection:
    def __init__(self, loop):
        self.loop = loop

    async def do_something(self):
        # Check if the current loop is the same as the one with which the
        #  connection was created
        if asyncio.get_event_loop() is not self.loop:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "This connection is being used with a different event loop!")
        return "Success"


@pytest_asyncio.fixture(scope="module", loop_scope="module")
async def async_connection():
    """Set up a async connection object with module scope."""
    event_loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    print(f"Setting up fixture: event_loop_id {id(event_loop)}")
    connection = FakeAsyncConnection(event_loop)
    yield connection


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use module loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_2(async_connection):
    """Use module loop again"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="function")
async def test_use_function_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use function loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="This connection is being used with a different event loop!"):
        # This should raise an error because the connection is being used with a different loop
        await async_connection.do_something()

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I need to understand exactly what's happening. Let me trace through the issue:

1. When pytest collects tests, it creates scoped event loop fixtures for each scope level (module, class, etc.)
2. When a test with loop_scope="module" runs, it gets the module-scoped event loop fixture
3. When a test with loop_scope="function" runs, it gets the function-scoped "event_loop" fixture
4. When another test with loop_scope="module" runs, it should get the same module-scoped event loop, BUT it's getting a different one

Looking at the code, I suspect the issue is in how the event loop fixtures are managed. Let me look at the key areas:

1. `_retrieve_scope_root` - finds the correct collector for a scope
2. Event loop fixture creation in `pytest_collectstart` 
3. How event loops are injected in `pytest_runtest_setup`

My hypothesis is that when a function-scoped test runs, it somehow interferes with the module-scoped event loop fixture. 

Looking at the stash mechanism:
- Each collector stores its event loop fixture ID in `collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id]`
- When a test needs a scoped event loop, it looks up the appropriate collector and gets the fixture ID from the stash

The problem might be that the stash entry is being overwritten or corrupted somehow.

Let me look more closely at how the event loop fixtures are created and managed...

Actually wait, let me think about this differently. The issue says that the final test gets a NEW loop id that's different from BOTH previous loop ids. That suggests that a new event loop fixture is being created somehow.

Let me trace through what should happen:
1. Module collector creates a scoped event loop fixture with ID like "test_file.py::<event_loop>" and stores it in stash
2. First module test uses that fixture - gets loop A
3. Function test uses the regular "event_loop" fixture - gets loop B  
4. Third module test should reuse the same fixture from step 1 - should get loop A again

But instead it's getting loop C - a completely new loop.

This suggests that the module-scoped event loop fixture is being recreated or the stash lookup is failing.

def pytest_collectstart(collector: pytest.Collector) -> None:
    try:
        collector_scope = next(
            scope
            for cls, scope in _fixture_scope_by_collector_type.items()
            if isinstance(collector, cls)
        )
    except StopIteration:
        return
    print(f"DEBUG: pytest_collectstart for {collector.nodeid}, scope={collector_scope}")
    # Session is not a PyCollector type, so it doesn't have a corresponding
    # "obj" attribute to attach a dynamic fixture function to.
    # However, there's only one session per pytest run, so there's no need to
    # create the fixture dynamically. We can simply define a session-scoped
    # event loop fixture once in the plugin code.
    if collector_scope == "session":
        event_loop_fixture_id = _session_event_loop.__name__
        collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id
        print(f"DEBUG: Stored session event loop fixture: {event_loop_fixture_id}")
        return
    # There seem to be issues when a fixture is shadowed by another fixture
    # and both differ in their params.
    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/2043
    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/11350
    # As such, we assign a unique name for each event_loop fixture.
    # The fixture name is stored in the collector's Stash, so it can
    # be injected when setting up the test
    event_loop_fixture_id = f"{collector.nodeid}::<event_loop>"
    collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id
    print(f"DEBUG: Stored scoped event loop fixture for {collector.nodeid}: {event_loop_fixture_id}")

    @pytest.fixture(
        scope=collector_scope,
        name=event_loop_fixture_id,
    )
    def scoped_event_loop(
        *args,  # Function needs to accept "cls" when collected by pytest.Class
        event_loop_policy,
    ) -> Iterator[asyncio.AbstractEventLoop]:
        new_loop_policy = event_loop_policy
        with _temporary_event_loop_policy(new_loop_policy):
            loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()
            loop.__pytest_asyncio = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            asyncio.set_event_loop(loop)
            print(f"DEBUG: Created new scoped event loop: {id(loop)} for fixture {event_loop_fixture_id}")
            yield loop
            print(f"DEBUG: Closing scoped event loop: {id(loop)}")
            loop.close()

    # @pytest.fixture does not register the fixture anywhere, so pytest doesn't
    # know it exists. We work around this by attaching the fixture function to the
    # collected Python object, where it will be picked up by pytest.Class.collect()
    # or pytest.Module.collect(), respectively
    if type(collector) is Package:
        # Packages do not have a corresponding Python object. Therefore, the fixture
        # for the package-scoped event loop is added to a stack. When a module inside
        # the package is collected, the module will attach the fixture to its
        # Python object.
        __package_loop_stack.append(scoped_event_loop)
    elif isinstance(collector, Module):
        # Accessing Module.obj triggers a module import executing module-level
        # statements. A module-level pytest.skip statement raises the "Skipped"
        # OutcomeException or a Collector.CollectError, if the "allow_module_level"
        # kwargs is missing. These cases are handled correctly when they happen inside
        # Collector.collect(), but this hook runs before the actual collect call.
        # Therefore, we monkey patch Module.collect to add the scoped fixture to the
        # module before it runs the actual collection.
        def _patched_collect():
            # If the collected module is a DoctestTextfile, collector.obj is None
            module = collector.obj
            if module is not None:
                module.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop
                try:
                    package_loop = __package_loop_stack.pop()
                    module.__pytest_asyncio_package_scoped_event_loop = package_loop
                except IndexError:
                    pass
            return collector.__original_collect()

        collector.__original_collect = collector.collect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        collector.collect = _patched_collect  # type: ignore[method-assign]
    elif isinstance(collector, Class):
        collector.obj.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>@pytest.hookimpl
def pytest_collectstart(collector: pytest.Collector) -> None:
    try:
        collector_scope = next(
            scope
            for cls, scope in _fixture_scope_by_collector_type.items()
            if isinstance(collector, cls)
        )
    except StopIteration:
        return
    # Session is not a PyCollector type, so it doesn't have a corresponding
    # "obj" attribute to attach a dynamic fixture function to.
    # However, there's only one session per pytest run, so there's no need to
    # create the fixture dynamically. We can simply define a session-scoped
    # event loop fixture once in the plugin code.
    if collector_scope == "session":
        event_loop_fixture_id = _session_event_loop.__name__
        collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id
        return
    # There seem to be issues when a fixture is shadowed by another fixture
    # and both differ in their params.
    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/2043
    # https://github.com/pytest-dev/pytest/issues/11350
    # As such, we assign a unique name for each event_loop fixture.
    # The fixture name is stored in the collector's Stash, so it can
    # be injected when setting up the test
    event_loop_fixture_id = f"{collector.nodeid}::<event_loop>"
    collector.stash[_event_loop_fixture_id] = event_loop_fixture_id

    @pytest.fixture(
        scope=collector_scope,
        name=event_loop_fixture_id,
    )
    def scoped_event_loop(
        *args,  # Function needs to accept "cls" when collected by pytest.Class
        event_loop_policy,
    ) -> Iterator[asyncio.AbstractEventLoop]:
        new_loop_policy = event_loop_policy
        with _temporary_event_loop_policy(new_loop_policy):
            loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()
            loop.__pytest_asyncio = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            asyncio.set_event_loop(loop)
            yield loop
            loop.close()

    # @pytest.fixture does not register the fixture anywhere, so pytest doesn't
    # know it exists. We work around this by attaching the fixture function to the
    # collected Python object, where it will be picked up by pytest.Class.collect()
    # or pytest.Module.collect(), respectively
    if type(collector) is Package:
        # Packages do not have a corresponding Python object. Therefore, the fixture
        # for the package-scoped event loop is added to a stack. When a module inside
        # the package is collected, the module will attach the fixture to its
        # Python object.
        __package_loop_stack.append(scoped_event_loop)
    elif isinstance(collector, Module):
        # Accessing Module.obj triggers a module import executing module-level
        # statements. A module-level pytest.skip statement raises the "Skipped"
        # OutcomeException or a Collector.CollectError, if the "allow_module_level"
        # kwargs is missing. These cases are handled correctly when they happen inside
        # Collector.collect(), but this hook runs before the actual collect call.
        # Therefore, we monkey patch Module.collect to add the scoped fixture to the
        # module before it runs the actual collection.
        def _patched_collect():
            # If the collected module is a DoctestTextfile, collector.obj is None
            module = collector.obj
            if module is not None:
                module.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop
                try:
                    package_loop = __package_loop_stack.pop()
                    module.__pytest_asyncio_package_scoped_event_loop = package_loop
                except IndexError:
                    pass
            return collector.__original_collect()

        collector.__original_collect = collector.collect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        collector.collect = _patched_collect  # type: ignore[method-assign]
    elif isinstance(collector, Class):
        collector.obj.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/pytest-dev__pytest-asyncio__0.25/pytest_asyncio/plugin.py has been edited.    942	def _provide_clean_event_loop() -> None:
   943	    # At this point, the event loop for the current thread is closed.
   944	    # When a user calls asyncio.get_event_loop(), they will get a closed loop.
   945	    # In order to avoid this side effect from pytest-asyncio, we need to replace
   946	    # the current loop with a fresh one.
   947	    # Note that we cannot set the loop to None, because get_event_loop only creates
   948	    # a new loop, when set_event_loop has not been called.
   949	    policy = asyncio.get_event_loop_policy()
   950	    new_loop = policy.new_event_loop()
   951	    policy.set_event_loop(new_loop)

   808	        return

def pytest_generate_tests(metafunc: Metafunc) -> None:
    marker = metafunc.definition.get_closest_marker("asyncio")
    if not marker:
        return
    scope = _get_marked_loop_scope(marker)
    print(f"DEBUG: pytest_generate_tests for {metafunc.definition.nodeid}, scope={scope}")
    if scope == "function":
        return
    event_loop_node = _retrieve_scope_root(metafunc.definition, scope)
    event_loop_fixture_id = event_loop_node.stash.get(_event_loop_fixture_id, None)
    print(f"DEBUG: Found event_loop_fixture_id: {event_loop_fixture_id}")

    if event_loop_fixture_id:
        # This specific fixture name may already be in metafunc.argnames, if this
        # test indirectly depends on the fixture. For example, this is the case
        # when the test depends on an async fixture, both of which share the same
        # event loop fixture mark.
        if event_loop_fixture_id in metafunc.fixturenames:
            print(f"DEBUG: Fixture already in metafunc.fixturenames")
            return
        fixturemanager = metafunc.config.pluginmanager.get_plugin("funcmanage")
        assert fixturemanager is not None
        if "event_loop" in metafunc.fixturenames:
            raise MultipleEventLoopsRequestedError(
                _MULTIPLE_LOOPS_REQUESTED_ERROR.format(
                    test_name=metafunc.definition.nodeid,
                    scope=scope,
                    scoped_loop_node=event_loop_node.nodeid,
                ),
            )
        # Add the scoped event loop fixture to Metafunc's list of fixture names and
        # fixturedefs and leave the actual parametrization to pytest
        # The fixture needs to be appended to avoid messing up the fixture evaluation
        # order
        print(f"DEBUG: Adding fixture {event_loop_fixture_id} to metafunc")
        metafunc.fixturenames.append(event_loop_fixture_id)
        metafunc._arg2fixturedefs[event_loop_fixture_id] = (
            fixturemanager._arg2fixturedefs[event_loop_fixture_id]

        # the "_provide_clean_event_loop" finalizer of the event_loop fixture
        # will already have installed a fresh event loop, in order to shield
        # subsequent tests from side-effects. We close this loop before restoring
        # the old loop to avoid ResourceWarnings.
        try:
            current_loop = _get_event_loop_no_warn()
            print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Current loop is {current_loop}, closing it")
            current_loop.close()
        except RuntimeError:
            print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] No current loop to close")
            pass
        print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Restoring old loop {old_loop}")

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement

Based on all my investigation, here's what I've discovered:

### 5.1 What the problem is:
When module-scoped tests with `loop_scope="module"` are mixed with function-scoped tests, the module-scoped event loop fixture is not being reused correctly. Instead, subsequent module tests get a different event loop than the one originally created by the module fixture.

### 5.2 Where the problem is located:
The issue is in pytest's fixture execution mechanism, specifically triggered by the interaction between:
1. Our dynamically created scoped event loop fixtures 
2. The finalizers of function-scoped `event_loop` fixtures
3. Specifically the `_provide_clean_event_loop` finalizer

### 5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
1. Module test 1: Our fixture executes, creates loop A, sets it as current
2. Function test: Standard fixture executes, creates loop B, finalizer runs `_provide_clean_event_loop` which creates loop C and sets it as current
3. Module test 2: `request.getfixturevalue()` returns loop C directly instead of executing our fixture to get loop A

### 5.4 Best practices for the fix:
- Minimal changes to existing code
- Don't break existing functionality  
- Ensure proper fixture scoping semantics
- Handle the interaction between different fixture scopes correctly

### 5.5 How to fix the problem:
The core issue is that `request.getfixturevalue()` is somehow bypassing our fixture execution and returning the current event loop directly.

Looking at the debug output, the problem occurs in `_asyncgen_fixture_wrapper` where:
```python
event_loop = request.getfixturevalue(event_loop_fixture_id)
```

Instead of executing our fixture, this returns the wrong loop.

The root cause appears to be that the `_provide_clean_event_loop` finalizer is setting a new event loop as current, and somehow this is affecting pytest's fixture resolution mechanism.

However, after careful analysis, I believe the real issue is more subtle. Looking at the code more carefully, I think the problem is in how we handle the fixture lifecycle.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Clear Problem Statement (Revised)

### 5.1 What the problem is:
When module-scoped tests with `loop_scope="module"` are mixed with function-scoped tests, the module-scoped event loop fixture is not being reused correctly. Instead of reusing the originally created event loop, subsequent module tests get a different event loop created by the function test's finalizer.

### 5.2 Where the problem is located:
The issue is in the interaction between:
1. Our dynamically created scoped event loop fixtures
2. The finalizers of function-scoped `event_loop` fixtures, specifically `_provide_clean_event_loop`

### 5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
1. **Module test 1**: Our fixture executes, creates loop A, sets it as current, test runs on loop A
2. **Function test**: Standard fixture executes, creates loop B, finalizer runs `_provide_clean_event_loop` which creates loop C and sets it as current
3. **Module test 2**: `request.getfixturevalue()` returns loop C directly instead of executing our fixture to get loop A

### 5.4 Root cause analysis:
The root cause is that `_provide_clean_event_loop` creates a new event loop and sets it as current. When subsequent tests call `request.getfixturevalue()` for our scoped fixture, pytest's fixture resolution mechanism somehow bypasses our fixture and returns the current event loop directly.

This suggests there's a fallback mechanism in pytest or pytest-asyncio where if fixture resolution somehow fails or is cached incorrectly, it falls back to using the current event loop.

### 5.5 How to fix the problem:
The fix should ensure that our scoped fixtures are always executed when requested, regardless of what other fixtures do. The issue seems to be in pytest's fixture caching/resolution mechanism being affected by the state changes from other fixtures.

Looking at the code more carefully, I believe the fix is to ensure that our fixture results are properly isolated from the global event loop state changes.

   732	    # @pytest.fixture does not register the fixture anywhere, so pytest doesn't
   733	    # know it exists. We work around this by attaching the fixture function to the
   734	    # collected Python object, where it will be picked up by pytest.Class.collect()
   735	    # or pytest.Module.collect(), respectively
   736	    if type(collector) is Package:
   737	        # Packages do not have a corresponding Python object. Therefore, the fixture
   738	        # for the package-scoped event loop is added to a stack. When a module inside
   739	        # the package is collected, the module will attach the fixture to its
   740	        # Python object.
   741	        __package_loop_stack.append(scoped_event_loop)
   742	    elif isinstance(collector, Module):
   743	        # Accessing Module.obj triggers a module import executing module-level
   744	        # statements. A module-level pytest.skip statement raises the "Skipped"
   745	        # OutcomeException or a Collector.CollectError, if the "allow_module_level"
   746	        # kwargs is missing. These cases are handled correctly when they happen inside
   747	        # Collector.collect(), but this hook runs before the actual collect call.
   748	        # Therefore, we monkey patch Module.collect to add the scoped fixture to the
   749	        # module before it runs the actual collection.
   750	        def _patched_collect():
   751	            # If the collected module is a DoctestTextfile, collector.obj is None
   752	            module = collector.obj
   753	            if module is not None:
   754	                module.__pytest_asyncio_scoped_event_loop = scoped_event_loop
   755	                try:
   756	                    package_loop = __package_loop_stack.pop()
   757	                    module.__pytest_asyncio_package_scoped_event_loop = package_loop
   758	                except IndexError:
   759	                    pass
   760	            return collector.__original_collect()

   338	                # asyncio.run() creates a new event loop without closing the existing
   339	                # one. For any test, but the first one, this leads to a ResourceWarning
   340	                # when the discarded loop is destroyed by the garbage collector.
   341	                # We close the current loop to avoid this
   342	                try:
   343	                    asyncio.get_event_loop().close()
   344	                except RuntimeError:
   345	                    pass
   346	                return asyncio.run(asyncio.sleep(0))
   347	                # asyncio.run() sets the current event loop to None when finished
   348	
   349	            @pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
   350	            # parametrization may impact fixture ordering
   351	            @pytest.mark.parametrize("n", (0, 1))
   352	            async def test_does_not_fail(sets_event_loop_to_none, n):
   353	                pass
   354	            """
   355	        )
   356	    )
   357	    result = pytester.runpytest("--asyncio-mode=strict")
   358	    result.assert_outcomes(passed=2)

Actually, let me create a test that matches our failing scenario exactly:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pytest-dev__pytest-asyncio__0.25/test_exact_scenario.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>import pytest_asyncio
import asyncio
import pytest


class FakeAsyncConnection:
    def __init__(self, loop):
        self.loop = loop

    async def do_something(self):
        # Check if the current loop is the same as the one with which the
        #  connection was created
        if asyncio.get_event_loop() is not self.loop:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "This connection is being used with a different event loop!")
        return "Success"


@pytest_asyncio.fixture(scope="module", loop_scope="module")
async def async_connection():
    """Set up a async connection object with module scope."""
    event_loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
    print(f"Setting up fixture: event_loop_id {id(event_loop)}")
    connection = FakeAsyncConnection(event_loop)
    yield connection


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use module loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_2(async_connection):
    """Use module loop again"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    result = await async_connection.do_something()
    assert result == "Success"


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="function")
async def test_use_function_scope_loop_1(async_connection):
    """Use function loop"""
    print(f"Test using loop with id: {id(asyncio.get_event_loop())}")
    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError, match="This connection is being used with a different event loop!"):
        # This should raise an error because the connection is being used with a different loop
        await async_connection.do_something()


@pytest.mark.asyncio(loop_scope="module")
async def test_use_module_scope_loop_3(async_connection):
    """Unexpectedly fail to use module scope again"""

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION: The Solution

The issue is in how pytest handles fixture caching when fixtures are dynamically created and attached to modules.

When our module-scoped fixture executes for test 1:
1. It creates loop A and yields it
2. Test 1 runs on loop A
3. Fixture finalizes, but the `_provide_clean_event_loop` finalizer from the function test has already set loop C as current
4. Our fixture's context manager tries to restore the old state, but gets confused

The key insight is that when test 3 runs, `request.getfixturevalue()` somehow thinks our fixture already executed and yielded a result, so it returns a cached value instead of calling our fixture.

But what if the cached value got corrupted?

Actually, let me look at this from a completely different angle.

What if the issue is that our fixture somehow shares some state with the function fixture?

Actually, let me look at the issue more carefully.

Looking at the debug output when all tests run:

Test 1:
- Our fixture called, creates loop A (`138896014403856`)
- Test uses loop A

Function test:
- Runs normally

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION: The Actual Solution

The issue is in pytest's fixture caching mechanism. When fixtures are dynamically created and attached to modules, there can be issues with how pytest tracks and caches their results.

The specific problem is that when the function test's finalizer `_provide_clean_event_loop` runs, it creates a new event loop and sets it as current. This somehow affects pytest's internal state such that when test 3 requests our fixture, pytest thinks it already has the result cached and returns the current event loop instead of executing our fixture.

The fix is to ensure that our scoped fixtures are properly isolated from these global state changes.

Looking at the code, I think the issue is in how we handle the fixture lifecycle. Specifically, in our `_temporary_event_loop_policy` context manager.

Let me examine the issue more carefully.

When our fixture runs for test 1:
1. It enters `_temporary_event_loop_policy` context
2. It creates and sets its own loop
3. It yields the loop
4. Test 1 runs
5. Function test runs and its finalizer creates loop C and sets it current
6. Our fixture tries to exit the context manager, but now the "current loop" is loop C, not our loop A
7. Our fixture closes loop C (oops!) and tries to restore the old loop

This corruption of state is what's causing the issue.

But actually, let me look at the debug output more carefully:

```
DEBUG: [FIXTURE_CALL] Closing scoped event loop: 138896014403856
DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Restoring old policy <asyncio.unix_events._UnixDefaultEventLoopPolicy object at 0x7e533f97a580>
DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Current loop is <_UnixSelectorEventLoop running=False closed=False debug=False>, closing it
DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Restoring old loop <_UnixSelectorEventLoop running=False closed=False debug=False>
```

The "current loop" that gets closed is NOT our loop `138896014403856`. It's some other loop that's still open.

This suggests that between when our fixture yielded and when it finalized, the current event loop was changed by the function test's finalizer.

So when test 3 runs, the pytest fixture system is somehow in a corrupted state.

The fix should ensure that our fixture properly isolates itself from these global state changes.

Let me look at the `_temporary_event_loop_policy` context manager:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pytest-dev__pytest-asyncio__0.25/pytest_asyncio/plugin.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[768, 795]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/pytest-dev__pytest-asyncio__0.25/pytest_asyncio/plugin.py:

def _temporary_event_loop_policy(policy: AbstractEventLoopPolicy) -> Iterator[None]:
    old_loop_policy = asyncio.get_event_loop_policy()
    try:
        old_loop = _get_event_loop_no_warn()
    except RuntimeError:
        old_loop = None
    print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Saving old policy {old_loop_policy} and loop {old_loop}")

    try:
        yield
    finally:
        print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Restoring old policy {old_loop_policy}")

        # When a test uses both a scoped event loop and the event_loop fixture,
        # the "_provide_clean_event_loop" finalizer of the event_loop fixture
        # will already have installed a fresh event loop, in order to shield
        # subsequent tests from side-effects. We close this loop before restoring
        # the old loop to avoid ResourceWarnings.

   781	        print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Closing our loop: {id(loop)}")
   782	        loop.close()
   783	        print(f"DEBUG: [_temporary_event_loop_policy] Restoring old loop {old_loop}")
   784	        asyncio.set_event_loop(old_loop)
   785	
   786	
   787	_REDEFINED_EVENT_LOOP_FIXTURE_WARNING = dedent(
   788	    """\
   789	
Review the changes and make sure they are as expected. Edit the file again if necessary.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>
